The two parameters that show whether the treatment worked are the blood glucose rising above 70 mg/dL and the diaphoresis and shakiness resolving, since both directly confirm the hypoglycemia has been corrected.
Case Study CJ-B-REDU-1

A 34-year-old client is admitted to the medical-surgical unit following a motor vehicle collision with reported blunt abdominal trauma. On admission, vital signs are blood pressure 118/76 mmHg, heart rate 92/min, respiratory rate 18/min, and temperature 37.0 degrees Celsius. Two hours later, the client reports increasing abdominal pain and the practical nurse notes the abdomen is now firm and distended.

  1. The correct answer is A: a new firm, distended abdomen with increasing pain following blunt abdominal trauma is the most concerning finding because it suggests active internal bleeding, an emergency that was not present on admission. The admission heart rate of 92/min was within a normal range and not itself alarming. A respiratory rate of 18/min is normal. The client's report of pain at admission was already known and does not represent a new, worsening finding.
  2. The correct answer is D: a new firm, distended abdomen with increasing pain after blunt abdominal trauma is most consistent with internal hemorrhage from an injured abdominal organ. Paralytic ileus typically develops after abdominal surgery, not blunt trauma, and would not usually cause a firm, distended abdomen with pain this early. A wound infection requires a surgical incision, which this client does not have. Urinary retention would cause suprapubic distention rather than the diffuse firm distention described here.
  3. The correct answer is B: a rising heart rate to 128/min together with a falling blood pressure to 88/54 mmHg reflects the body's compensatory response to significant blood loss and confirms the client is developing hypovolemic shock. A blood pressure of 116/74 mmHg is within normal limits and does not support this hypothesis. A respiratory rate of 16/min is normal and not a shock indicator. A temperature of 37.2 degrees Celsius is normal and unrelated to hemorrhage.
  4. The correct answer is A: given the signs of possible internal hemorrhage and developing shock, the nurse's first action should be to notify the registered nurse and prepare for a possible rapid response so the client can be evaluated and treated urgently. Encouraging ambulation could worsen bleeding and is unsafe for a client with suspected hemorrhage. Applying heat to the abdomen does not address the underlying emergency and could increase bleeding. Waiting until the next scheduled vital sign check dangerously delays recognition and treatment of a life-threatening complication.
  5. The correct answer is C: while waiting for the registered nurse, the priority is to keep the client positioned supine and continue close monitoring of vital signs to detect further deterioration. Offering oral fluids is inappropriate because the client may need surgery and should remain NPO. Assisting the client to ambulate could worsen internal bleeding and increase oxygen demand. Removing IV access would eliminate the route needed for emergency fluids or blood products.
  6. The correct answer is B: a rising blood pressure to 110/70 mmHg with a decreasing heart rate toward 90/min indicates the client's circulatory status is stabilizing and improving. An abdomen that remains firm and distended would suggest ongoing bleeding rather than improvement. A heart rate continuing to rise to 132/min would indicate worsening, not improving, shock. Becoming less responsive to verbal stimuli would be a sign of worsening, not improving, perfusion.
Case Study CJ-B-REDU-2

A 71-year-old client with a history of chronic kidney disease undergoes a CT scan with intravenous contrast for evaluation of abdominal pain. Baseline serum creatinine before the scan was 1.4 mg/dL. Twenty-four hours later, the client's urine output has decreased and the practical nurse is reviewing new laboratory results as part of routine post-procedure monitoring.

  1. The correct answer is D: a urine output of only 20 mL over an hour is far below the expected minimum and is the most concerning finding in a client with chronic kidney disease who recently received IV contrast. Mild fatigue is a nonspecific, common complaint. A slightly decreased appetite is also nonspecific and not directly concerning. A blood pressure of 122/78 mmHg is within normal limits.
  2. The correct answer is A: a client with pre-existing chronic kidney disease who develops decreased urine output after receiving IV contrast is at classic risk for contrast-induced acute kidney injury. A urinary tract infection would not typically be triggered by IV contrast administration. Dehydration from fasting alone would not fully explain the temporal relationship to the contrast administration in a client with this risk profile. A decreased urine output this significant is not a normal, expected response to the procedure.
  3. The correct answer is B: a serum creatinine rising from a baseline of 1.4 mg/dL to 2.6 mg/dL confirms worsening kidney function and supports the hypothesis of contrast-induced acute kidney injury. A sodium of 140 mEq/L is within normal limits and does not reflect kidney filtration. A hemoglobin of 13.8 g/dL is within a normal range and unrelated to renal function. A white blood cell count of 7,200/mm3 is within normal limits and does not indicate worsening kidney injury.
  4. The correct answer is C: increasing intravenous fluids as prescribed helps support renal perfusion and can help prevent further kidney injury in a client with early signs of contrast-induced nephropathy. Scheduling another contrast scan would place the kidneys at further risk and is inappropriate at this time. Restricting oral fluids would worsen, not improve, kidney perfusion. A nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug can further impair kidney function and should be avoided in this client.
  5. The correct answer is A: continuing to closely monitor and document hourly urine output is the priority nursing action while awaiting further orders, since it provides essential data on the client's evolving kidney function. Encouraging independent ambulation does not address the priority concern and is not the most important action right now. Removing the urinary catheter would eliminate the ability to accurately track hourly output. Offering a high-sodium meal is inappropriate for a client with worsening renal function and possible fluid retention.
  6. The correct answer is B: an increase in urine output to 45 mL per hour indicates improving kidney perfusion and function after treatment. A serum creatinine continuing to rise to 3.1 mg/dL would indicate the kidney injury is worsening, not improving. Urine output remaining unchanged at 20 mL per hour would indicate no improvement. New lower extremity edema would suggest worsening fluid retention, not improvement.
Case Study CJ-B-REDU-3

A 58-year-old client is 2 days postoperative following a bowel resection. The practical nurse's morning assessment reveals a temperature of 38.9 degrees Celsius, heart rate of 118/min, respiratory rate of 24/min, and blood pressure of 96/58 mmHg. The client reports feeling unusually weak and the surgical incision appears more reddened than it did the previous day.

  1. The correct answer is A: the combination of fever, tachycardia, and hypotension together represents the systemic inflammatory and hemodynamic changes that define early sepsis and is the most concerning finding pattern. The client's report of feeling weak alone is a nonspecific symptom. Increased incisional redness alone, without the systemic vital sign changes, is a more localized finding. An isolated respiratory rate of 24/min without the other findings is less specific on its own.
  2. The correct answer is D: fever, tachycardia, hypotension, and increasing incisional redness together are most consistent with early sepsis originating from a surgical site infection. Normal postoperative healing would not include a fever this high, hypotension, or worsening incisional redness. Anxiety related to hospitalization does not explain objective findings such as fever and hypotension. Dehydration from reduced oral intake alone would not typically cause a fever or worsening incisional erythema.
  3. The correct answer is C: a further drop in blood pressure to 82/50 mmHg along with new confusion indicates the client's perfusion and mental status are worsening, supporting progression toward septic shock. A urine output of 50 mL per hour is within a normal range and reassuring. Being alert and oriented times four is a reassuring finding, not one suggesting worsening shock. Scant, serosanguineous incisional drainage is an expected postoperative finding, not a sign of progression to shock.
  4. The correct answer is C: notifying the registered nurse immediately and anticipating blood cultures with IV fluids addresses the urgent need to identify the infectious source and begin treatment for sepsis. Applying a warm compress to the incision does not address the systemic infection and delays appropriate treatment. Encouraging independent ambulation is inappropriate given the client's hypotension and instability. Waiting 4 hours for the next scheduled assessment dangerously delays recognition and treatment of a life-threatening condition.
  5. The correct answer is D: continuing to closely monitor vital signs and reassess the incision is the priority nursing action while awaiting further orders, since it provides ongoing data to detect further deterioration. Restricting oral fluids is inappropriate for a client who may need increased fluid resuscitation. Discontinuing IV access would eliminate the route needed for emergency fluids or antibiotics. Allowing the client to ambulate alone to the bathroom is unsafe given the hypotension and instability.
  6. The correct answer is A: a decreasing temperature to 37.3 degrees Celsius along with a rising blood pressure to 108/68 mmHg indicates the client's infection and hemodynamic status are improving in response to treatment. A heart rate continuing to rise to 128/min would indicate worsening, not improving, status. A blood pressure continuing to fall to 80/48 mmHg would indicate worsening shock. Increasing incisional redness would suggest the infection is not responding to treatment.
  7. The correct answer is B: hypokalemia increases myocardial sensitivity to digoxin and significantly raises the risk of digoxin toxicity, making a potassium of 2.9 mEq/L the most concerning result in a client taking furosemide without potassium replacement. A sodium of 138 mEq/L is within normal limits and unrelated to digoxin toxicity risk. A calcium of 9.4 mg/dL is a normal value and does not increase digoxin sensitivity. A glucose of 110 mg/dL is a normal fasting value and does not affect digoxin sensitivity.
  8. The correct answer is A: the INR directly reflects the degree of anticoagulation from warfarin, so it must be reviewed before each dose to confirm the client is within the safe therapeutic range and not at risk for bleeding. Serum creatinine reflects kidney function and does not guide warfarin dosing directly. White blood cell count reflects infection or immune status, unrelated to anticoagulation. Serum albumin reflects nutritional and protein status and is not used to guide warfarin administration.
  9. The correct answer is B: increasing tightness of the neck dressing along with new difficulty swallowing suggests an expanding hematoma compressing the airway, a surgical emergency after thyroidectomy that must be reported immediately. Mild hoarseness is a common, expected finding after thyroid surgery from local swelling or nerve irritation. A pain rating of 3 out of 10 is well controlled and not concerning. A temperature of 37.4 degrees Celsius is within a normal postoperative range.
  10. The correct answer is D: a low pH combined with an elevated PaCO2 and a normal bicarbonate indicates the primary problem is retention of carbon dioxide from inadequate ventilation, which defines respiratory acidosis. Metabolic acidosis would show a low bicarbonate rather than a normal one. Metabolic alkalosis would show an elevated pH and elevated bicarbonate, not present here. Respiratory alkalosis would show an elevated pH with a low PaCO2, the opposite of this result.
  11. The correct answer is C: a sudden increase in bright red drainage of this volume suggests active postoperative hemorrhage and must be reported immediately. Gentle bubbling in the water seal chamber during exhalation is an expected finding related to normal air movement. Serosanguineous drainage of 30 mL over an hour is within an expected postoperative range. Fluctuation, or tidaling, of the fluid level with respirations is expected and confirms the system is patent.
  12. The correct answer is D: the therapeutic range for lithium is generally 0.6 to 1.2 mEq/L, so a level of 0.9 mEq/L falls safely within that range. A level of 0.2 mEq/L is subtherapeutic and unlikely to control symptoms effectively. Levels of 1.8 mEq/L and 2.4 mEq/L are both above the therapeutic range and place the client at risk for lithium toxicity.
  13. The correct answer is C: cloudy effluent combined with new abdominal tenderness is a classic early sign of peritonitis, a serious complication of peritoneal dialysis that requires prompt reporting. Clear, pale yellow effluent is the expected appearance. An outflow volume approximately equal to the inflow volume indicates the exchange is functioning as expected. Draining within the expected dwell time is also an expected, reassuring finding.
  14. The correct answer is B: a rapidly expanding, firm swelling at the femoral site suggests active bleeding or hematoma formation and must be reported immediately to prevent significant blood loss or limb compromise. A palpable, equal pedal pulse is a reassuring sign of adequate circulation. Mild soreness at the insertion site is an expected postprocedure finding. A small area of ecchymosis is a common, expected finding after an arterial puncture.
  15. The correct answer is A: pain that continues to increase despite analgesia and worsens with passive stretching of the toes is a hallmark early sign of compartment syndrome and requires immediate reporting. Mild toe swelling at the cast edge is a common, expected finding after cast application. Capillary refill under 2 seconds indicates adequate perfusion and is reassuring. A cast that feels snug but comfortable, with no other concerning findings, does not by itself suggest compartment syndrome.
  16. The correct answer is B: a blood urea nitrogen and serum creatinine both elevated above normal reference ranges together indicate that the kidneys are not adequately filtering waste products, consistent with impaired renal function. These values are well above what would be expected with normal renal function. Liver dysfunction is more directly reflected by elevated liver enzymes and bilirubin, not BUN and creatinine. Dehydration alone can raise BUN somewhat but would not typically cause this degree of creatinine elevation.
  17. The correct answer is C: an ACE inhibitor such as lisinopril and a potassium-sparing diuretic such as spironolactone both reduce potassium excretion, so combining them increases the risk of dangerous hyperkalemia. Hyponatremia is more commonly associated with thiazide or loop diuretics, not this combination. Hypokalemia would be expected with a potassium-wasting diuretic, the opposite mechanism of spironolactone. Hypercalcemia is not a typical effect of either medication.
  18. The correct answer is A: sequential compression devices along with active ankle pump exercises promote venous return and reduce blood stasis, directly lowering the risk of deep vein thrombosis after major orthopedic surgery. Strict bed rest for several days actually increases venous stasis and DVT risk rather than reducing it. Massaging the operative leg is contraindicated because it could dislodge an existing clot. A high Fowler's position does not specifically reduce DVT risk and is not indicated for this purpose.
  19. The correct answer is D: new chills, back pain, and a temperature rise this soon after starting a transfusion are classic signs of an acute hemolytic or febrile transfusion reaction and require the nurse to stop the transfusion immediately. Resting quietly with stable vital signs is a reassuring finding. Mild warmth at the IV site alone, without other systemic signs, is not a reaction indicator. Simply asking about the timing of the transfusion is not a clinical finding requiring the transfusion to stop.
  20. The correct answer is D: drinking coffee within the required fasting window is an NPO violation that increases the client's risk for aspiration under anesthesia, so the registered nurse and surgical team must be notified before proceeding. Continuing with routine preparation as planned ignores a safety risk that could delay or change the surgical plan. Giving additional fluids would further violate NPO status and increase risk. Simply documenting the finding without notifying the team fails to address the immediate safety concern.
  21. The correct answer is C: vaginal leaking of clear fluid together with decreased fetal movement suggests amniotic fluid leakage and possible fetal compromise, both of which require immediate reporting after an amniocentesis. Mild cramping that resolves with rest is a common, expected finding. A small amount of clear fluid on the puncture site dressing is expected local drainage, not vaginal leaking. Feeling tired after an invasive procedure is a common, nonspecific finding that does not require urgent reporting.
  22. The correct answer is B: troponin is a cardiac-specific protein released when myocardial cells are damaged, so an elevated level in a client with chest pain most strongly indicates myocardial injury such as an acute myocardial infarction. Chronic kidney disease is reflected by BUN and creatinine, not troponin. Liver inflammation is reflected by AST, ALT, and bilirubin. Skeletal muscle strain would more likely elevate creatine kinase without a corresponding troponin rise.
  23. The correct answer is A: diaphoresis, trembling, and confusion result from the sympathetic nervous system response and impaired brain glucose supply that occur with hypoglycemia, and together they form a classic recognizable pattern. Fruity breath odor with deep, rapid respirations describes Kussmaul breathing seen in diabetic ketoacidosis, a hyperglycemic condition. Increased thirst and frequent urination are classic signs of hyperglycemia, not hypoglycemia. Warm, dry skin with slow respirations does not match the sympathetic response typical of hypoglycemia.
  24. The correct answer is B: a popping sensation followed by a sudden increase in clear pink drainage suggests wound dehiscence, and the priority is to cover any exposed tissue with a sterile saline-soaked dressing while notifying the registered nurse immediately. Applying a plain dry dressing and continuing routine care fails to protect exposed tissue or address the emergency. Simply reinforcing the dressing and waiting for the next shift delays necessary intervention for a surgical emergency. Ambulating the client would increase intra-abdominal pressure and could worsen the dehiscence.
  25. The correct answer is C: these findings describe autonomic dysreflexia, a life-threatening emergency in clients with a spinal cord injury above T6, and the priority is to sit the client upright to lower blood pressure while quickly identifying and removing the triggering stimulus, most often a distended bladder or impacted bowel. Lowering the head of the bed would worsen the elevated blood pressure. Giving a routine scheduled antihypertensive does not address the urgent triggering stimulus and delays appropriate action. Waiting for the next scheduled vital sign check delays treatment of a hypertensive emergency.
  26. The correct answer is A: a white blood cell count well above the normal range typically indicates the body is responding to an active infectious or inflammatory process. Chronic anemia is reflected by a low hemoglobin and hematocrit, not an elevated white blood cell count. A bleeding disorder is more directly reflected by abnormal platelet counts or coagulation studies. Dehydration alone can mildly concentrate blood values but does not typically cause this degree of white blood cell elevation.
  27. The correct answer is D: a headache that worsens with sitting and improves when lying flat is a classic sign of a post-lumbar puncture cerebrospinal fluid leak, and the nurse should keep the client flat while notifying the registered nurse. Encouraging the client to sit up and ambulate would worsen the headache and does not address the underlying cause. Restricting fluids is inappropriate, since increased fluid intake is often encouraged to help replace cerebrospinal fluid volume. Simply reassuring the client without further action fails to address a finding that needs to be reported.
  28. The correct answer is C: the therapeutic range for digoxin is generally 0.5 to 2.0 ng/mL, so a level of 1.1 ng/mL falls safely within that range. Levels of 3.5 ng/mL and 2.8 ng/mL are both well above the therapeutic range and place the client at significant risk for digoxin toxicity. A level of 0.1 ng/mL is far below the therapeutic range and unlikely to provide adequate therapeutic effect.
  29. The correct answer is A: a palpable thrill and an audible bruit indicate turbulent blood flow through the fistula and confirm that it is patent and functioning appropriately. A cool, pale hand compared to the other side suggests inadequate blood flow, possibly from steal syndrome. Numbness and tingling in the fingers can also indicate compromised circulation from steal syndrome and should be reported. An absent thrill or bruit suggests the fistula may be clotted and requires immediate reporting.
  30. The correct answer is B: sudden shortness of breath with diminished breath sounds on the affected side suggests a pneumothorax, a known complication of thoracentesis that requires immediate reporting. Mild soreness at the puncture site is an expected, minor finding. A small amount of serous drainage on the dressing is a common, expected finding after the procedure. Resting comfortably with stable vital signs is a reassuring finding that does not require urgent reporting.
  31. The correct answer is D: severe hyperkalemia of this degree is a cardiac emergency that can cause life-threatening dysrhythmias, so the priority is to assess the cardiac rhythm for peaked T waves or a widened QRS complex. Bowel sounds are not the priority assessment for a critical potassium value, though hyperkalemia can affect gastrointestinal motility. Pupillary response is unrelated to potassium's primary effect on cardiac conduction. Peripheral edema reflects fluid status, not the immediate cardiac risk from hyperkalemia.
  32. The correct answer is D: facial swelling, wheezing, and a sudden significant drop in blood pressure after IV contrast indicate anaphylaxis, a life-threatening emergency requiring immediate help and epinephrine per protocol. Simply documenting and continuing routine monitoring dangerously delays treatment of a rapidly progressing emergency. Repositioning the client and waiting 10 minutes does not address the immediate airway and circulatory compromise. Offering comfort measures does not treat the underlying anaphylactic reaction.
  33. The correct answer is A: an elevated serum ammonia level occurs when a diseased liver cannot adequately metabolize ammonia produced by the gut, and the resulting buildup crosses into the brain to cause the confusion and lethargy of hepatic encephalopathy. An elevated sodium level is not the typical cause of these neurologic changes in cirrhosis. A decreased potassium level does not directly explain worsening confusion in this context. An elevated hemoglobin level is not associated with hepatic encephalopathy and is uncommon in cirrhosis, where anemia is more typical.
  34. The correct answer is B: a significantly elevated blood pressure combined with a new headache and visual disturbances are hallmark signs of preeclampsia and must be reported promptly. Mild ankle swelling at the end of the day is a common, expected finding in later pregnancy. A weight gain of half a pound over a week is within an expected range. A blood pressure of 118/74 mmHg is within normal limits and not concerning.
  35. Fluid overload is assessed through weight, intake and output, and lung sounds, not coagulation studies.
  36. A hypoglycemic reaction would present with a low, not elevated, blood glucose. Respiratory alkalosis would show an elevated, not decreased, pH. The hyperosmolar hyperglycemic state typically occurs without significant ketosis or acidosis, unlike the low pH seen here.
  37. The correct answer is B: fingers that are pale, cool, and without sensation suggest impaired circulation and nerve compression, both signs of a neurovascular emergency that require immediate reporting. Fingers that move freely with strong grip strength are a reassuring sign of intact motor function.
  38. The correct answer is D: hypercalcemia slows smooth muscle activity in the gastrointestinal tract, commonly causing constipation and decreased bowel sounds. Hyperactive deep tendon reflexes are more characteristic of hypocalcemia, not hypercalcemia. Positive Trousseau and Chvostek signs indicate neuromuscular irritability seen with hypocalcemia, the opposite imbalance. Muscle twitching and tetany also occur with hypocalcemia rather than an elevated calcium level.
  39.   40.   41.   42. The correct answer is D: AST and ALT are enzymes released from damaged liver cells, so a significant elevation in both indicates hepatocellular injury.   68.   70.   71. The correct answer is C: a fasting blood glucose of 88 mg/dL falls within the normal reference range of about 70 to 100 mg/dL, so this result reflects a normal fasting glucose level. Practice Test 10 Answer Key

  1. The correct answers are Blank 1: B and Blank 2: A: a platelet count of 35,000/mm3 is well below the normal reference range and, combined with new bruising and bleeding gums, places the client at risk for spontaneous bleeding. Platelet counts of 250,000, 400,000, or 180,000/mm3 are within the normal range and would not explain these bleeding symptoms. A low platelet count increases bleeding risk rather than promoting clot formation, infection, or fluid overload.
  2. The correct answer is D: accurate orthostatic vital signs require measuring blood pressure and heart rate while the client is supine for at least 5 minutes first, then sitting, then standing, with 1 to 3 minutes between each position before comparing all three sets of readings. Option A reverses the order by having the client stand before the supine measurement is taken. Option B starts correctly but skips the sitting position before standing. Option C begins by comparing readings before any measurements have actually been obtained, which is not possible.
  3. The correct answer is A: systematic rhythm strip interpretation follows the order of determining rate, assessing rhythm regularity, evaluating P waves, measuring the PR interval, and finally measuring the QRS complex width. Option B reverses the first two steps, assessing rhythm before determining rate. Option C reverses the entire sequence, starting with QRS width and ending with rate. Option D evaluates the PR interval before assessing rhythm and P waves, which is out of the standard sequence.
  4. The correct answer is B: preparation for a lumbar puncture begins with verifying that a signed informed consent form is on the chart, followed by having the client void, positioning the client in the fetal lateral recumbent position, and instructing the client to remain still during needle insertion. Option A has the client void before consent is verified, which is out of order. Option C positions the client before consent is verified and before voiding. Option D positions the client before the client has voided, which is also out of order.
  5. The correct answer is A: when a pulse oximeter reading suddenly drops, the nurse should first recheck the reading to verify accuracy, then assess the client's respiratory status and level of consciousness, apply supplemental oxygen per protocol, and finally notify the registered nurse or provider. Option B skips rechecking the reading and assesses the client first, which risks acting on an inaccurate value. Option C applies oxygen before assessing the client. Option D notifies the registered nurse before even verifying that the low reading is accurate.
  6. The correct answer is C: when new calf swelling and warmth are found, the nurse should first assess the extremity, then measure and document calf circumference for comparison, keep the client on bed rest while avoiding massage, and finally notify the registered nurse with complete data. Option A notifies the registered nurse before the assessment is complete. Option B places bed rest before the circumference measurement is obtained. Option D measures circumference before a full assessment of the extremity has been performed.
  7. The correct answers are A, B, and C: new spreading bruising at the femoral access site, a weaker pedal pulse on the affected side, and new numbness and tingling in the affected foot together suggest bleeding or a vascular complication at the catheterization site and must be reported immediately. A stable heart rate of 78 beats per minute and regular rhythm, the client's ability to move all extremities, and the client's denial of chest pain are all reassuring findings that do not require urgent reporting.
  8. The correct answers are A, B, and C: sudden sharp chest pain with worsening shortness of breath, a drop in oxygen saturation from 96% to 89%, and absent breath sounds on the affected side together are classic signs of a pneumothorax following a thoracentesis and require immediate reporting. A dry, intact puncture site dressing, the client's ability to speak in full sentences, and a normal temperature of 37.0 degrees Celsius are all reassuring findings that do not indicate this complication.
  9. The correct answers are A, B, and C: a temperature of 38.4 degrees Celsius, an absolute neutrophil count of 400/mm3 indicating severe neutropenia, and new chills with shaking together indicate a possible neutropenic fever, a medical emergency requiring immediate reporting in a chemotherapy client. Mild fatigue present since treatment began, warm and dry skin without a rash, and a fair appetite with tolerated small meals are all expected findings that do not require urgent reporting.
  10. The correct answers are Row 1: Expected, Row 2: Unexpected, Row 3: Expected, Row 4: Unexpected, Row 5: Expected: mild bloating and gas passage, drowsiness after conscious sedation, and a small streak of blood on the first stool are all expected after a routine colonoscopy. A rigid, board-like abdomen with severe pain suggests bowel perforation, and continuous bright red rectal bleeding suggests active hemorrhage, both of which are unexpected and require immediate reporting.
  11. The correct answers are Row 1: Expected, Row 2: Unexpected, Row 3: Expected, Row 4: Unexpected, Row 5: Expected: mild soreness at the site, positioning on the right side to apply pressure, and stable baseline vital signs are all expected after a liver biopsy.   12. The correct answers are Row 1: Unexpected, Row 2: Unexpected, Row 3: Expected, Row 4: Unexpected, Row 5: Expected: a temperature rise with chills, flank pain with dark cola-colored urine, and hives with itching are all transfusion reaction signs that are unexpected and require the transfusion to be stopped and reported.   13. The correct answers are Condition/Cue 1: A, Condition/Cue 2: A, Priority Action: A, Expected Outcome/Parameter 1: A, Expected Outcome/Parameter 2: A: a blood pressure of 82/50 mmHg and increasing abdominal distention together suggest internal bleeding after abdominal surgery, so the priority action is to notify the registered nurse or provider immediately and prepare for fluid resuscitation rather than continuing routine care. The two parameters that show whether this action worked are the blood pressure returning toward the client's baseline and the heart rate decreasing toward baseline, since both directly reflect whether circulating volume has been restored.
  14. The two parameters that show whether this action worked are the absence of new bleeding or bruising and a rising platelet count on repeat labs, since both directly reflect whether the bleeding risk has improved.
Case Study CJ-B-PHYS-1

A 74-year-old client is 2 days post-operative following a total hip arthroplasty. The client suddenly reports sharp chest pain that worsens with breathing and new shortness of breath while sitting in the bedside chair. Vital signs are temperature 37.2 degrees Celsius, heart rate 128/min, respiratory rate 32/min, blood pressure 96/58 mmHg, and oxygen saturation 87% on room air. The client appears anxious and diaphoretic.

  1. The correct answer is D: an oxygen saturation of 87% on room air represents an immediate, life-threatening oxygenation problem that must be addressed before other findings, since inadequate oxygenation can rapidly lead to organ damage. The elevated heart rate and low blood pressure are important but are more likely secondary responses to the hypoxemia and the underlying complication. The client's anxiety is also a secondary symptom rather than the priority finding to address first.
  2. The correct answer is B: sudden pleuritic chest pain, tachycardia, and hypoxemia occurring 2 days after hip replacement surgery are the classic presentation of a pulmonary embolism, a known complication of reduced mobility after orthopedic surgery. A wound infection would more typically present with fever and localized incision changes rather than sudden chest pain and hypoxemia. An anxiety attack and a hip dislocation would not explain this specific combination of pleuritic chest pain and dropping oxygen saturation.
  3. The correct answer is D: reduced mobility following recent hip replacement surgery is a well-established major risk factor for venous stasis and clot formation, making pulmonary embolism the most likely complication in this client. The client's age alone is a minor contributing factor compared with recent immobility. Recent use of the bedside chair and the client's anxiety level are not established risk factors for this complication.
  4. The correct answer is B: applying supplemental oxygen and positioning the client upright addresses the immediate hypoxemia and improves the client's ability to breathe while further evaluation and treatment are arranged. Ambulating the client would increase oxygen demand and could worsen the client's condition. Administering scheduled pain medication does not address the acute respiratory compromise, and removing compression stockings would not help and could increase the risk of further clot mobilization.
  5. The correct answer is A: after initiating supplemental oxygen, the nurse's next priority is to notify the registered nurse or provider immediately so that further diagnostic testing and treatment for the suspected pulmonary embolism can begin without delay. Documenting and reassessing in 2 hours would dangerously delay needed intervention for a potentially life-threatening condition. Returning the client to bed and offering a blanket are comfort measures that do not address the urgency of the situation.
  6. The correct answer is C: an oxygen saturation improving to 95% on supplemental oxygen indicates that gas exchange is improving and the client is responding appropriately to treatment. A heart rate increasing further to 140 beats per minute and a blood pressure decreasing further to 80/46 mmHg would both indicate worsening, not improvement. A respiratory rate increasing further to 36 breaths per minute would also indicate the client's respiratory status is deteriorating rather than improving.
  7. The correct answer is B: a serum potassium level of 2.9 mEq/L is dangerously low and must be reported before continuing an insulin infusion, since insulin drives potassium further into cells and can precipitate life-threatening hypokalemia and cardiac dysrhythmias. A decreasing blood glucose trend during treatment is expected and desired. Thirst and dry mucous membranes are consistent with the client's underlying dehydration from diabetic ketoacidosis, and an hourly urine output of 45 mL is within an acceptable range and does not require immediate escalation.
  8.
